Thus a correctly implemented log-sum-exp has gradient
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0269em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>,
including at the level of the full stabilized expression. The
cancellation must not be assumed for modified code. If
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mop mtight"><span class="mtight">m</span><span class="mtight">a</span><span class="mtight">x</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
is reused in another branch, detached in only one of its two
occurrences, rounded, or combined with clipping, its gradient
contribution need not cancel. Such code should retain the appropriate
dependence on
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mop mtight"><span class="mtight">m</span><span class="mtight">a</span><span class="mtight">x</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
or supply a custom backward rule. A fused log-sum-exp primitive is the
safe choice when the complete identity is intended.</p>
<p>Log-sum-exp stabilization does not remove the conditioning problem
created by very small
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span>,
so small-regularization computations may still need more iterations or
an
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span>-scaling
schedule.</p>
<p>The parameter
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span>
controls a statistical and numerical tradeoff. As
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">↓</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>,
the regularized objective approaches the unregularized transport
objective and the coupling becomes more concentrated on low-cost edges.
At the same time, the kernel develops a larger dynamic range and the
scaling problem becomes harder. Larger
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span>
produces a smoother coupling and a better-conditioned computation, but
introduces more entropic bias.</p>
<h3 id="differentiating-through-the-solution">Differentiating through
the solution</h3>
<p>Let
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5678em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">π</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
be the converged coupling. A general scalar loss may depend on both the
optimized transport value and the coupling:
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="25" id="eq:general-sinkhorn-loss"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span aria-hidden="true">(27)</span></span> The first term is
obtained directly. The only issue considered below is how to compute the
second term, which requires the response
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mop"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathrm">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span></span></span></span>
of the converged plan. The stationarity equation and the two marginal
equations are

class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><span class="equation-number" aria-hidden="true">(28)</span></span> Within the inner Sinkhorn
optimization,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span></span></span></span>,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span>,
and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span></span></span></span>
are held fixed while
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span></span></span></span>,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0257em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1076em;">f</span></span><span style="top:-3.2634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.0833em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1944em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>,
and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7622em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">g</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.2222em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1944em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
are solved. In the outer differentiation, an input may vary through an
upstream parameter
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">θ</span></span></span></span>;
for example,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">θ</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
gives

Thus <em>fixed</em> refers only to the inner optimization. If
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span></span></span></span>
and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span>
are fixed ensemble weights, set
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mop"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathrm">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mop"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathrm">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>
while retaining
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mop"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathrm">d</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span></span></span></span>.

<h2 id="wasserstein-gradient-flow">Wasserstein gradient flow</h2>
<p>Optimal transport fixes both endpoint distributions and minimizes the
cost of moving mass between them. A gradient flow fixes only the present
distribution. The next distribution must be chosen. An energy functional
selects where it moves, and the Wasserstein distance measures how much
mass must be moved.</p>
<p>This is the distributional version of ordinary gradient descent. Let
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5782em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
and let
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
be differentiable and convex. The backward-Euler step for
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6679em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6679em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1111em;"><span class="mord">˙</span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
can be written in either of the equivalent forms

<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="36" id="eq:wasserstein-two"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span is the transport analogue of Euclidean distance.</p>
<p>The Jordan–Kinderlehrer–Otto (JKO) scheme makes two replacements in
Eq. <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:euclidean-minimizing-movement">(35)</a>:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span></span></span></span>
becomes a probability density
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span></span></span></span>,
and Euclidean distance becomes
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> <span class="citation">[<a href="#ref-jko-1998">7</a>]</span>:
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="37" id="eq:jko-step"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
is fixed and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span></span></span></span>
is the possible endpoint of the next step. The functional
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0993em;">F</span></span></span></span>
chooses among these endpoints. The transport term penalizes moving too
far from
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
in one time step.</p>
<p>For overdamped particles in a potential
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
with diffusion constant
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span>,
the probability density
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
has free energy
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="38" id="eq:fokker-planck-free-energy"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0993em;">F</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord descent, the probability moves with velocity
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">v</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0993em;">F</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>
and obeys
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0556em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">⋅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">v</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>.

Fokker–Planck equation. The potential energy produces the drift term,
and the entropy produces diffusion.</p>
<p>Its stationary density is
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7167em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">eq</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∝</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.888em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.888em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mord mtight">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>.
Consequently,
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="40" id="eq:free-energy-relative-entropy"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0993em;">F</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" gives
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0361em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span Thus Eq. <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:jko-step">(37)</a> with the free energy in
Eq. <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:fokker-planck-free-energy">(38)</a>
is an implicit time discretization of the Fokker–Planck evolution.</p>
<p>Only the object being evolved changes in the field-theory version of
Cotler and Rezchikov <span class="citation">[<a href="#ref-cotler-rezchikov-2022">8</a>]</span>. Instead of
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>,
they evolve the normalized probability functional
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">Λ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>
on field configurations. The energy is the relative entropy
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">KL</span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">Λ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">∥</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">Q</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">Λ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>,
and the Wasserstein transport takes place in configuration space.</p>
<h2 id="the-schrödinger-bridge">The Schrödinger bridge</h2>
<p>Let
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0785em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span><span class="mrel mtight">≤</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span><span class="mrel mtight">≤</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2452em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
be a complete history in the chosen state space, and let
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>
be a prescribed probability distribution over such histories. If the
observed endpoint marginals are
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>,
Schrödinger’s problem is to make the smallest deformation of
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>
that reproduces those observations <span class="citation">[<a href="#ref-schrodinger-1931">9</a>, <a href="#ref-leonard-2014">10</a>]</span>:
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="41" id="eq:schrodinger-bridge"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">KL</span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" optimization acts on
a distribution of complete histories, not on one path or on endpoint
moments. The state space and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span></span></span></span>
define the problem; no equation of motion for
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span></span></span></span>
is required. Figure <a href="#fig:schrodinger-reweighting">1</a> shows the bridge
reweighting the histories already allowed by
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span></span></span></span>.</p>
<figure id="fig:schrodinger-reweighting">
<img src="/notes/optimal-transport/schrodinger-reweighting.svg" alt="Reference trajectories with a central terminal mode are reweighted into bridge trajectories with two outer terminal modes." />
<figcaption>The Schrödinger bridge reweights histories already allowed
by the reference distribution. Line thickness and endpoint-circle size
represent probability weight. The reference predicts most mass near the
center; the observation requires the bridge to move that weight to the
two outer modes.</figcaption>
</figure>
<p>Equation <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:schrodinger-bridge">(41)</a> is
the Schrödinger bridge. Both endpoint distributions are fixed exactly;
the unknown is the probability distribution over complete histories
between them.</p>
<h3 id="entropy-regularization-in-path-space">Entropy regularization in
path space</h3>
<p>Assign each history a cost
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">C</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>.
With the endpoint marginals fixed, entropy-regularized transport
minimizes
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="42" id="eq:path-entropic-transport"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7387em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7387em;"><span style="top:-3.113em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing class="equation-number" aria-hidden="true">(42)</span></span> The cost term
concentrates
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span></span></span></span>
on cheap histories; the entropy term spreads
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span></span></span></span>
among them. Their competition occurs entirely within the set of path
distributions having marginals
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>.</p>

mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.3214em;"><span style="top:-2.314em;"><span class="pstrut" <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">Z</span></span></span></span>
normalizes
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>.
Direct substitution gives
<span class="equation-block" data-equation-numbers="44" id="eq:path-entropy-kl-identity"><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:2.2222em;vertical-align:-0.8622em;"></span><span class="mop op-symbol large-op" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">lo<span style="margin-right:0.0139em;">g</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">lo<span style="margin-right:0.0139em;">g</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">Z</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><span class="equation-number" aria-hidden="true">(44)</span></span> Since
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">lo<span style="margin-right:0.0139em;">g</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">Z</span></span></span></span>
is independent of
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">P</span></span></span></span>,
Eqs. <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:path-entropic-transport">(42)</a>
and <a class="equation-reference" href="#eq:schrodinger-bridge">(41)</a> have
the same minimizer. Thus
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>
is the Gibbs representation of the path cost. No reference equation of
motion is needed.</p>
<p>For the discrete transport problem, a history is the single move
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7167em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">y</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0359em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">j</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>,
with

mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span>.
Entropic optimal transport is therefore the one-step Schrödinger bridge,
and Sinkhorn computes its solution. With intermediate times, the same
variational principle acts on complete histories.</p>
<section class="references" aria-labelledby="optimal-transport-references-heading">
<h2 id="optimal-transport-references-heading">References</h2>
<ol>
<li id="ref-peyre-cuturi-2019">G. Peyré and M. Cuturi, <em>Computational Optimal Transport</em>,

applied to Latin squares, magic squares and Sudoku boards,”
arXiv:1610.04259, 2016.</li>
<li id="ref-cuturi-2013">M. Cuturi, “Sinkhorn distances: Lightspeed computation of optimal
transport,” in <em>Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems
26</em>, 2013.</li>
<li id="ref-sinkhorn-1964">R. Sinkhorn, “A relationship between arbitrary positive matrices and
doubly stochastic matrices,” <em>The Annals of Mathematical
Statistics</em>, 35(2):876–879, 1964.</li>
<li id="ref-xie-2025">F. Xie, “Deriving the gradients of some popular optimal transport
algorithms,” arXiv:2504.08722v3, 2025.</li>
<li id="ref-eisenberger-2022">M. Eisenberger, A. Toker, L. Leal-Taixé, F. Bernard, and D. Cremers,
“A unified framework for implicit Sinkhorn differentiation,” in
<em>Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and
Pattern Recognition</em>, pages 509–518, 2022.</li>
<li id="ref-jko-1998">R. Jordan, D. Kinderlehrer, and F. Otto, “The variational formulation
of the Fokker–Planck equation,” <em>SIAM Journal on Mathematical
Analysis</em>, 29(1):1–17, 1998.</li>
<li id="ref-cotler-rezchikov-2022">J. Cotler and S. Rezchikov, “Renormalization group flow as optimal
transport,” <em>Physical Review D</em>, 108:025003, 2023.</li>
<li id="ref-schrodinger-1931">E. Schrödinger, “Über die Umkehrung der Naturgesetze,”
<em>Sitzungsberichte der Preussischen Akademie der Wissenschaften,
Physikalisch-Mathematische Klasse</em>, pages 144–153, 1931.</li>
<li id="ref-leonard-2014">C. Léonard, “A survey of the Schrödinger problem and some of its
connections with optimal transport,” <em>Discrete and Continuous
Dynamical Systems A</em>, 34(4):1533–1574, 2014.</li>
